# Changelog

2026-09-19  R-486  Runtime — real sandbox lifecycle contract + E2B driver. First of a five-task sequence (R-486..R-490) toward the founder's "Full isolation: per-user processes/sandboxes" multi-tenancy direction. Preceded by dedicated research (delegated to a subagent, sourced): a codebase-state audit confirmed zero containerization exists anywhere in the agent-engine, `scripts/test.sh` has an active enforced gate blocking `agent-engine`/`redis`/`runner-manager`/`nats`/`temporal`/`kubernetes` as Compose services, `services/runner-manager/` is an empty placeholder, the Studio server is a single shared in-memory process with no tenant dimension, the Go control-plane always proxies to one fixed `AgentEngineURL`, Postgres has no per-tenant scheme, and the existing `runtime/` provider abstraction is a real, well-designed Protocol wrapping thin/untested cloud stubs; a platform comparison found every serious 2025-2026 AI app-builder running real server-side code (Vercel Sandbox, E2B, Fly, CodeSandbox) converged on Firecracker microVMs, or gVisor via a managed provider (Lovable→Modal) — only browser-only sandboxing (Bolt.new/StackBlitz WebContainers) sidesteps real server execution entirely, which doesn't fit this platform's Python/Go backend requirement; and a technology-tradeoff comparison (Firecracker, gVisor, Kata Containers, plain Docker/cgroups, Kubernetes multi-tenancy patterns) concluded self-hosting any of these from scratch is a multi-quarter platform-engineering effort a single-founder-plus-AI-agent team doesn't have headcount for. Presented to the founder via AskUserQuestion (a hard-gate decision: adopting any of these means a new paid external cloud dependency, not just an architecture pattern); the founder's answer: build real, pluggable drivers for multiple providers (E2B, Vercel Sandbox, Daytona) so they can be switched later by cost/speed/smoothness, plus one free option that runs directly in the browser with no cost or configuration (WebContainers) — this task proves the pattern end-to-end with the first provider before repeating it three more times. Design verified by direct source read before writing any code: the existing `RuntimeProvider`/`PreviewPlan` abstraction (`runtime/contracts.py`) is a pure *planner* — `preview_plan()` returns a description of local install/run commands plus a URL string, and executing it (`local.run_preview`) runs those commands on the *same* machine; the existing `CloudSandboxProvider` (`runtime/drivers.py`) stub reuses this exact shape with a hardcoded placeholder URL (`https://<id>.e2b.dev`) and never calls any provider's real API — this planning abstraction is correct for local execution and stays completely untouched, because a real cloud sandbox fundamentally needs actual create/get-a-real-id-and-url/later-kill lifecycle management, which a pure "plan" has no room for. New, additive `SandboxHandle` + `SandboxLifecycleProvider` (`runtime/contracts.py`): a `SandboxHandle` (`provider_id`, `sandbox_id`, `url`, `status`) and a Protocol with `create()`/`status()`/`kill()`, genuinely mirroring what a real remote resource needs, alongside (never replacing) the existing `RuntimeProvider`. New `runtime/sandbox_http.py`: a small, safe, stdlib-only JSON HTTP helper shared by every sandbox driver this sequence adds (E2B now; Vercel Sandbox/Daytona in R-487/R-488) — bounded response size, a finite timeout, and redirect rejection (an auth header must never be replayed to another host), a purpose-built and much smaller sibling of `model_gateway/cloud.py`'s own HTTP safety net (that file is LLM-completion-specific — streaming, retry-after pacing, finish-reason mapping — none of which a sandbox lifecycle call needs). New `runtime/e2b.py`'s `E2BSandboxProvider`, implementing the new contract for real, verified against E2B's actual documented REST API fetched directly from `docs.e2b.dev` (not assumed from training data, and cross-checked against a second, independent fetch to resolve a `/sandboxes` vs `/v2/sandboxes` inconsistency across their own doc pages by preferring the two mutually-consistent, directly-fetched pages): `POST https://api.e2b.app/sandboxes` with `{"templateID", "timeout"}` and an `X-API-Key` header returns `201` with a `sandboxID`; the public URL is built from E2B's own documented pattern `https://{port}-{sandboxID}.e2b.app`; `DELETE https://api.e2b.app/sandboxes/{sandboxID}` returns `204` on success. `templateID` defaults to E2B's own documented generic default (`"base"`, confirmed via a second search), overridable via a new `E2B_TEMPLATE_ID` env var — a real generated OmniStackAI app needs a custom template with the right toolchain pre-installed, a separate, later concern once a real account exists to build one against. The target→port mapping is reused, not duplicated: a new `_port_for_target()` helper calls the existing `LocalRuntimeProvider().preview_plan(app_dir, target)` and parses the port out of its returned `http://127.0.0.1:{port}` URL, keeping `local.py` the single source of truth and naturally inheriting its `UnsupportedRuntimeTargetError` for an unknown target. E2B's `GET /sandboxes/{id}` status response's exact field shape could not be independently verified from the fetched documentation pages — `status()` defensively looks for a `"state"` string field and falls back to keeping the handle's existing status when absent, an honest, documented gap rather than a guessed-and-hidden assumption. Activated by the existing `RUNTIME_SPECS["e2b"]`'s `E2B_API_KEY` env var (declared since an earlier task, unused until now) — the same "add a key, it activates" pattern every cloud LLM provider already uses. **No real `E2B_API_KEY` exists in this environment** (`.env` checked: absent; `.env.example` carries only the pre-existing name-only placeholder, now joined by a new `E2B_TEMPLATE_ID` placeholder) — every automated test is offline via an injected fake `OpenerDirector`-shaped transport, mirroring `model_gateway/cloud.py`'s own testable-opener pattern for its LLM adapters; real live-cloud verification against an actual E2B account is honestly not possible here and is not claimed, deferred to whenever the founder provides a real key, exactly matching this project's existing precedent for cloud LLM providers (Groq/Anthropic/etc. stay unverified-live until a real key is added, and `task verify` never depends on one). Not in scope for this task: wiring `E2BSandboxProvider` into the Studio's actual preview flow (`studio/preview.py`) or `_build_stream()` — this task proves the driver is real and correctly built against E2B's actual API; swapping what the Studio actually uses for preview is a separate, later decision once more than one provider exists to choose between (R-490's job). agent-engine `task verify`: **3,680 tests OK** (22 new: 9 in new `test_runtime_sandbox_http.py`, 13 in new `test_runtime_e2b.py`), 0 model/network calls — covering successful create/kill/status against hand-built E2B-shaped responses, a missing key raising before any request is sent (both `create()` and `kill()`), HTTP error mapping (a real E2B-shaped 401 `{"message","error_code"}` body, and a non-JSON 500 body falling back to a generic message), oversized/malformed response rejection, redirect rejection, an `E2B_TEMPLATE_ID` override, the target→port mapping for all four known targets plus the unsupported-target case, and the API key never appearing in a `SandboxHandle`'s `repr()`; the pre-existing `test_runtime.py` suite (the `RuntimeProvider`/local/bootstrap tests) passes completely unmodified, confirming this task's additions are genuinely additive and the existing (still-stubbed) `CloudSandboxProvider`/`sandbox_driver()` planning path is untouched. Repo-wide `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass; a new `scripts/test.sh` contract block asserts the new contract/driver files and symbols exist. NEXT: R-487 (Vercel Sandbox driver), R-488 (Daytona driver), R-489 (the free WebContainers browser-only option, client-side in the console, Node.js/frontend apps only — an honest, documented limitation since WebContainers cannot run Python or Go), and R-490 (a provider-selection surface exposing the founder's explicit "switch easily by cost/speed/smoothness" ask).

2026-09-19  R-485  Console — streaming build UI. Fast-follow to R-484 (backend SSE build streaming), closing the loop it opened: the console's own `/studio` chat now consumes `POST /jobs/build/stream` for its create-path, replacing the static "Building…" wait with real, live incremental progress. Design verified by direct source read: `studio-chat.tsx`'s create-path is already plain-prompt-only (`sendBuild()` only ever POSTed `{prompt}` — Solution Pack/Ecosystem/`hybrid_ui` builds only exist in the separate raw-HTML Studio page, never this Next.js console), so every build this component can trigger is exactly the kind R-484's streaming route already supports, with no build-kind branching needed here. New `streamBuildApp()` in `lib/control-plane.ts`: unlike every other function in that file (which awaits and returns parsed JSON via `callControlPlane<T>`), this returns the raw upstream `Response` unparsed, so the proxy route can pipe its body straight through without buffering it. New proxy route `app/api/jobs/build/stream/route.ts`: auth-gates via `getSessionToken()` (same 401-if-missing shape as every other proxy route), validates a non-blank `prompt` (same 400 shape as the existing `app/api/jobs/build/route.ts`), then returns `new Response(upstream.body, {status, headers})` — one pipe-through that correctly handles both real shapes the control-plane can return: a streamed `text/event-stream` success body, and a plain buffered `application/json` pre-stream rejection (Solution Pack/Ecosystem/`hybrid_ui`, a normal `400`) — neither this route nor the browser needs to branch on which one it got. `studio-chat.tsx` gains `sendBuildStream()`, replacing `sendBuild()` as the create-path handler: `fetch()` + manual `response.body.getReader()` framing (not `EventSource`, which cannot send a POST body) — buffers bytes, splits on `"\n\n"` event boundaries, and parses each frame's `data:` line as JSON, handling three real shapes verified against R-484's actual live output (not assumed): a `{"phase": "generating_ir", "delta": "..."}` frame (only its length is used, to drive a live character-count indicator — the raw streaming JSON text itself would render as visibly broken partial JSON to a non-technical user, and a real-time partial-JSON-aware renderer is a materially larger feature explicitly left out of scope here), a `{"phase": "done", ...}` frame (the final build result, identical rendering to what the old non-streaming `sendBuild()` already produced — workspace/tabs update unchanged), and a bare `{"credit_balance": ..., "credits_spent": ...}` frame with **no** `"phase"` key at all (the Go relay's trailing `event: credits` frame — confirmed live during R-484 that credits arrive as a *separate* trailing frame in the streaming path, never merged into `"done"`, unlike the non-streaming response's shape). A `{"phase": "error", ...}` frame surfaces as a normal chat error message. What's shown while streaming is deliberately not the raw IR text but an honest, live-ticking "Generating your app… (N characters so far)" indicator — proving genuine live activity (the actual product goal: an engaged, alive first response) without fabricating a fake progress percentage or showing broken partial JSON; replaced by the existing full result-card rendering the instant `"done"` arrives. The existing non-streaming `POST /jobs/build`/`app/api/jobs/build/route.ts`/`buildApp()` all remain exactly as they are, genuinely reachable via direct API use, simply no longer called by this one UI component now that its create-path streams; edit stays non-streaming, per R-484's own scope boundary (`sendEdit()` untouched). **No browser-automation tool was available this session** (checked via `ToolSearch` — only Figma/design tools surfaced) — verified instead via `next start` (the real production build) plus `curl` driven through a real, cookie-based login session (`app/api/auth/login/route.ts` sets a real httpOnly `omnistackai_session` cookie; every subsequent request carried it exactly as a browser would) — a faithful behavioral proxy for the browser exercising the identical code path (Route Handler → fetch → Go control-plane → agent-engine), with only the pixels-on-screen part unverified; this task adds no new visual/CSS surface (the character-count text reuses the existing `.chat-message--assistant`/`.spinner` styling from R-475/R-477 unchanged). `pnpm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (21 routes, 1 new: `/api/jobs/build/stream`); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass (3,658 agent-engine tests unaffected — no agent-engine or control-plane file touched by this task). Live manual smoke: logged in via `POST /api/auth/login` with the same real test user from R-484 (`r484-smoke@example.com`), capturing a real session cookie; `curl -N` through the full real path (Next.js proxy → Go control-plane → agent-engine) for `{"prompt":"Build a simple bookmarks manager app"}` showed genuine token-by-token `generating_ir` deltas arriving with real millisecond-scale gaps over ~9 real seconds of wall-clock time, `Content-Type: text/event-stream` correctly relayed by the new Route Handler, ending with a real `"phase": "done"` frame (174 files, entities `Bookmark, BookmarkTag, Folder, Tag, User`, a real git commit sha `317913cdaa02a91fbc44b02f2d0d53d8efc3284d`, real usage with `input_tokens: 1875, output_tokens: 3725`), followed by a real trailing `event: credits` frame (`credit_balance: 100, credits_spent: 0` — honestly zero, this environment's configured cloud model has no price-book entry, the same pre-existing fact already documented in R-484's own evidence) — confirming `sendBuildStream()`'s three real frame shapes all parse correctly against genuine server output, not a hand-constructed fixture; a real request with no session cookie returned a real `401 {"error":"not signed in"}`; a real request with no `prompt` returned a real `400 {"error":"prompt is required"}`, both rejected before any upstream call; a real follow-up edit on the build just created (`POST /api/jobs/build/2/edit`, `{"prompt":"add a health check endpoint"}`) produced a real diff (`"2 added, 4 modified, 0 deleted, 170 unchanged"`), a real second commit sha, and correct turn history spanning both the original build's and this edit's turns — confirming the edit path is completely unaffected by this task's changes. NEXT: per the founder's stated build order ("streaming first, then scope isolation properly"), properly SCOPE (not yet build) full per-user process/sandbox isolation as its own multi-task project — a materially different architecture decision needing explicit founder sign-off, given the agent-engine has zero containerization today and `scripts/test.sh` actively blocks adding it as a Compose service. Also pending, per the founder's final answers on deploy/stack breadth (not yet scoped into task contracts): multi-target Publish/deploy (Netlify one-click; Vercel/Cloudflare/self-host/GitHub-export as options), Node.js backend codegen support alongside the existing Python/Go, and starting mobile technology work (React Native as the near-term target, per this session's earlier competitor research).

2026-09-19  R-484  Backend — real-time build streaming (SSE). First of the founder's post-roadmap priorities ("streaming first, then scope isolation properly"), chosen after three parallel research passes covering competitor streaming architecture, per-user isolation scoping, and deploy/stack breadth. Replaces the one-shot blocking "Building…" wait with genuine incremental progress; scoped as backend only — console UI consumption is R-485, a fast-follow, mirroring this session's proven backend-then-UI split (R-476→R-477, R-478→R-479). Design verified by direct source read before writing code: `model_gateway/contracts.py`'s `ModelProvider` Protocol already declares `stream(request) -> AsyncIterator[StreamEvent]` and every real provider already implements it, but `_build()`'s entire call chain (`build_app_from_prompt` → `generate_ir` → `provider.generate()`) never once called it — the gap this task closes is entirely in the intake/build layer, not the model layer. Added purely additive streaming twins, leaving every existing non-streaming function untouched: `generate_ir_stream()` (`intake/nl_to_ir.py`, an async generator yielding raw text deltas from `provider.stream()` then the final `IntakeResult`), `build_app_from_prompt_stream()` (`intake/build_app.py`, streams the IR-generation deltas then does the existing `build_app_from_ir()`'s disk/git work synchronously once the IR completes), and `_build_stream()` (`studio/live_serve.py`, an async generator mirroring `_build()`'s plain-prompt-only branch — Solution Pack, Ecosystem, and `hybrid_ui=True` builds are explicitly, honestly rejected via a new `StreamingBuildNotSupportedError` rather than silently falling back or guessing, matching the exact scope precedent `_edit()`/R-476 already set). New `POST /api/build/stream` route in `studio/server.py`: rejects unsupported build kinds with a normal `400` before any streaming starts, then switches to Server-Sent Events (`Content-Type: text/event-stream`, manual `self.wfile.write()`+`flush()` per event over stdlib `http.server`'s connection-close-delimited body, zero new dependencies), driven via a small `asyncio.run()`-wrapped drain loop matching `_edit()`'s existing "sync handler, async model calls" pattern. New Go control-plane route `POST /jobs/build/stream` (`internal/jobs/handler.go`): because crediting requires the final event's usage (only known once the stream completes), the relay cannot inject `credits_spent`/`credit_balance` into an already-sent frame — instead it relays every upstream SSE frame verbatim via `http.Flusher` as it arrives (a new `readSSEFrame`/`parseSSEDataPayload` pair reads exactly one frame at a time without altering what gets forwarded), then appends one trailing `event: credits\ndata: {...}\n\n` frame once the upstream `"done"` event's usage is known and debited via the same `creditsForUsage` computation `proxyAndDebit` already uses — including the edge case where `"done"` carries no `usage` at all (still emits a `credits_spent: 0` frame, matching the non-streaming response's own always-present shape, distinguished internally via a separate `sawDone` boolean rather than conflating "no usage" with "stream never completed"); a debit failure after the stream has already started (headers already sent, so a plain `500` is no longer possible) surfaces as a named `credits_error` SSE frame instead, an honest partial-failure shape. **Two real bugs found and fixed by this task's own gates, not glossed over**: (1) the SSE route initially sent `Connection: keep-alive`, which `BaseHTTPRequestHandler.send_header` specially interprets as "keep this socket open" (`self.close_connection = False`) — since the response has no `Content-Length`/chunked framing, the client's only signal the body has ended is the connection closing, so this hung every real client indefinitely; caught immediately by the first HTTP-level streaming test via a real client socket timeout, fixed by sending `Connection: close` instead. (2) `RecordingProvider` (`model_gateway/recording.py`) — the real usage/cost-tracking wrapper that every production build call is routed through via `resolve_generation_provider_from_env` — had no `.stream()` method at all; every automated test mocks around this real wrapping (handing `_build_stream()` a raw stub provider directly), so this was invisible to `task verify` and only surfaced via the task's own required live `curl -N` smoke test, which failed immediately with `'RecordingProvider' object has no attribute 'stream'`; fixed by adding `RecordingProvider.stream()`, mirroring `generate()`'s own success/failure ledger-recording shape exactly, plus 4 new tests in `test_recording_provider.py` (both files added to the task's `allowed_paths` mid-task once the gap was found, following the same discipline as every prior mid-task scope correction this session). agent-engine `task verify`: **3,658 tests OK** (27 net-new: 9 in `test_intake_nl_to_ir.py`, 3 in `test_build_app.py`, 11 in `test_studio_server.py`, 4 in `test_recording_provider.py`), 0 model/network calls — every new function exercised only against in-memory stub/stream providers. Control-plane `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...`: all green, 7 new tests in `handler_test.go` (55 total), covering verbatim frame relay + real debit after `done`, a `credits` frame still appended (charging 0) when `done` carries no usage, a mid-stream `error` frame relayed with no trailing `credits` frame, pre-stream Solution Pack/Ecosystem/`hybrid_ui` rejections proxied unchanged, upstream-unreachable → 502, and a debit failure surfacing as `credits_error`. Repo-wide `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` and a new `scripts/test.sh` contract block all pass. Live manual smoke against the real Docker control-plane (rebuilt via `task control-plane:verify`) and a freshly restarted real agent-engine Studio server (Python doesn't hot-reload — the first attempt against the still-running pre-fix process is exactly what surfaced bug #2 above): a real `curl -N` session through the real Go control-plane (not directly against the agent-engine) showed genuine token-by-token `generating_ir` deltas arriving with real millisecond-scale gaps over ~9 real seconds of wall-clock time, ending with a real `"phase": "done"` frame carrying the full real build result (177 files, entities `Tag, TaskComment, TaskItem, TaskTag, TodoList, User`, a real git commit sha, real `usage` with `input_tokens: 1875, output_tokens: 3144`), followed by a real trailing `event: credits` frame (`credit_balance: 100, credits_spent: 0` — honestly zero because this environment's configured cloud model has no price-book entry, a pre-existing, unrelated fact already documented in R-472/R-476's own evidence); all three unsupported build kinds (`pack_id`, `ecosystem_id`, `hybrid_ui`) confirmed cleanly rejected with a `400` and `Content-Type: application/json` before any SSE framing began. The existing non-streaming `POST /jobs/build`/`POST /api/build` were not touched by any edit in this task and remain covered unchanged by every pre-existing passing test. NEXT: R-485 (console: streaming build UI, consuming this endpoint in `studio-chat.tsx`'s `sendBuild()`), then properly scope (not yet build) full per-user process/sandbox isolation per the founder's stated build order.

2026-09-19  R-483  Fix — dynamic-route slug collision & duplicate FK identifier in the edit-delta path. Second follow-up task after the 7-task Phase D roadmap shipped, per the founder's "complete one by one all" direction. Fixes a real, reproducible bug found live during R-481's own manual smoke test (documented in `.ai/tasks/R-481.md`'s Gates & Evidence): (1) a Next.js dev-server crash (`Error: You cannot use different slug names for the same dynamic path ('counterId' !== 'counter_id')`) caused by a follow-up edit producing a new endpoint whose `{param}` path segment was spelled differently than an existing endpoint's own `{param}` for the same resource; (2) a duplicate `lib/types.ts` identifier (`TS2300`/`TS2687`/`TS2717`) caused by an edit's proposed entity declaring both an explicit field and a same-named to-one relation for the same conceptual foreign key. Root cause verified by direct source investigation before writing any fix, not assumed: the full-build prompt (`intake/nl_to_ir.py`) has no explicit casing rule for API path `{param}`s (camelCase only by its own worked example in `application_ir/examples.py`), while the edit-delta prompt (`intake/app_delta.py`) explicitly told the model to use `lower_snake_case` for the *entire* path including `{param}` placeholders — nothing in `parse_app_delta_proposal` or `apply_app_delta` reconciled a new endpoint's param spelling against an existing endpoint's own param for the same resource (`apply_app_delta` never even calls `normalize_ir()`, confirmed only the full-build path does). Separately, `codegen/nextjs.py`'s `_entity_interface()` (the `lib/types.ts` generator) computes a `declared` set of existing field names but only ever consults it for `created_at`/`updated_at`/`created_by` — for every to-one relation it unconditionally appended `f"{relation.name}_id"` with no check against `declared`; `Entity.__post_init__` validates field-name uniqueness and relation-name uniqueness as two separate sets, never cross-checking that a relation's implied FK column doesn't collide with an already-declared field. Confirmed this exact shape (an entity with both an explicit `post_id`-style field and a same-named relation) already sat, unexercised for this, in `tests/test_app_delta.py`'s own `_VALID_DELTA` fixture — both defects are deterministic, systemic code/prompt facts, not one-off model randomness, and reproduce reliably for any entity/resource name. Fixed at the structural root, not just the two known call sites: `ApiEndpoint.__post_init__` (`application_ir/ir.py`) now canonicalizes every `{param}` to camelCase unconditionally via a new pure, idempotent `_canonicalize_path_params()` helper, applied at the one place every `ApiEndpoint` is ever constructed — fresh build, edit delta, solution packs, ecosystem packs, and any future caller — closing the bug class structurally. Both prompts also corrected to ask for camelCase `{param}`s and explicit reuse of an existing resource's param name, as a first line of defense (the code-level fix is the real guarantee, not reliant on the model following instructions). `_entity_interface()` now skips the synthesized `<relation>_id` field when a same-named field is already explicitly declared — the explicit field's own declaration always wins. A pre-existing test (`test_form_update_screens.py`'s `test_nextjs_web_adapter_generates_complete_project` and `test_subcollection_view_renders_new_child_link`) initially failed after the fix — investigated, not reverted: its fixture's own hardcoded `ApiEndpoint` path used the raw `{article_id}` spelling, and the previously-passing assertion matched that exact raw spelling in the generated query-string link. Confirmed this was never a functional dependency on snake_case specifically: the generated reader code (`comment_editor/page.tsx`) already defensively checks THREE possible spellings (`article_id`, then `articleId`, then `article`), with `articleId` as its own second-priority fallback — clear pre-existing evidence this exact inconsistency was already anticipated by the generator's own reader code. Updated both assertions to expect the new, correct, canonical `articleId=` camelCase output rather than reverting the fix; the fixture's own raw `{article_id}` literal was deliberately left unchanged so the test continues to exercise the real canonicalization end-to-end. Six new regression tests added: `ApiPathParamCanonicalizationTests` (5 cases in `test_application_ir.py`, including one reproducing the exact live bug scenario) and `DuplicateForeignKeyFieldTests` (1 case in `test_nextjs_adapter.py`, asserting the generated `lib/types.ts` contains the FK identifier exactly once). Gates: agent-engine `task verify` — **Ran 3,635 tests, OK** (6 new); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass. Live (real Docker control-plane already running, a freshly restarted real agent-engine Studio server in preview mode with the fix, real console already running) — **reproduced the exact originally-reported scenario end to end**: `POST /jobs/build` with the identical original prompt ("A simple counter app where users track a single number that goes up or down") → real 157-file build; `POST /jobs/build/1/edit` with the identical original follow-up prompt ("add a reset button that sets the counter back to zero") → real second commit; `POST /jobs/build/1/preview` → real `200 {"status":"ready",...}`, **no crash this time** — confirmed by grepping the agent-engine's own real log for the exact original error string (`"different slug names"` / `"Failed to reload dynamic routes"`), genuinely absent; listed the real generated app's dynamic route directories on disk → exactly **one** (`apps/web/app/counters/[counterId]`), no colliding sibling `[counter_id]` folder; `POST /jobs/build/1/problems` → a real `tsc` run reported 8 real errors, **zero of them duplicate-identifier errors (no TS2300/TS2687/TS2717) and zero of them in `lib/types.ts`** — the 8 remaining are pre-existing, unrelated LLM-synthesized-UI issues in `app/page.tsx` (the same class already honestly documented as out-of-scope noise in R-480's/R-481's own live smokes, not this task's concern); read the real generated `lib/types.ts` directly → the `Counter` interface has exactly one `user_id?: string;` line, no duplicate — confirmed the fix live, not just via the compiler. NEXT (per the founder's "complete one by one all"): R-484 (real-time build/edit streaming, renumbered from R-483 now that this bug fix took that number), per-user backend multi-tenancy, and Publish/deploy each involve a materially different architecture decision (transport protocol, tenancy model, deploy target) requiring explicit founder direction before implementation, per the standing "stop and ask for hard-gate architecture decisions" rule.

2026-09-19  R-482  Model Provider settings UI. First follow-up task after the 7-task Phase D roadmap shipped, per the founder's "complete one by one all" direction (2026-09-19): continue through every named follow-up, one Tracker ID at a time, full discipline. A real, live Dyad-style provider status page. Verified before writing any code: `model_gateway/overview.py`'s `platform_overview()` already existed (real, tested, metadata-only — never a key value, only a boolean derived from key-env presence) but was only ever used to generate a static, build-time JSON snapshot (`task console:snapshot` → `apps/console-web/data/overview.json`) consumed by the existing public, unauthenticated `/fabric` marketing/architecture page — no live HTTP endpoint served this anywhere. New, never surfaced anywhere before: `intake/provider_resolution.py`'s `resolve_generation_provider_from_env()` always succeeds (it falls through to local Ollama at the end, never raises for "nothing configured") and its returned `ModelProvider` has a real `.provider_id` property — calling it is a safe, side-effect-free way (aside from one real but lightweight ≤1.5s Ollama reachability ping) to report exactly which provider would run the *next* real build/edit right now. New `_provider_status()` in `studio/live_serve.py` returns `platform_overview()`'s dict augmented with `activeNow: {providerId, modelId} | null` and `activeNowError: string | null`. New zero-arg `GET /api/providers` route in `studio/server.py` (new `ProvidersFn` type, new `providers_fn` param, mirroring the existing zero-arg `/api/preview` route's dispatch shape) — wired into `live_serve.py`'s always-on `control_kwargs` (build-only mode included: needs only env vars and one optional local ping, never a toolchain or running preview, the same reasoning already established for `edit_fn`/`turns_fn`/`problems_check_fn`). New control-plane route `GET /jobs/providers`, auth-required, **no credit debit** (a status read, not a model call), plain `proxyGet`. New console additions: `lib/control-plane.ts`'s `ProviderStatus`/`ProviderInfo` types + `getProviderStatus()`, new proxy route `app/api/providers/route.ts`, new authenticated `app/settings/layout.tsx` + `app/settings/page.tsx` (auth gate mirrors `app/studio/layout.tsx`'s exact pattern), a new `SettingsIcon` (gear) in `studio-icons.tsx`, linked from the Studio topbar (a new icon-button next to `LogoutButton`) and the home page's existing link list. The Settings page shows a live "Ready"/"Not ready" active-now callout at the top, then the same Provider table shape `/fabric` already established (`badge on/off` for Active/Needs key) — reusing the established visual language, now live-polled instead of a static snapshot; `/fabric` itself, `data/overview.json`, and `task console:snapshot` are all untouched, a deliberately separate, existing surface. **A real bug was found AND FIXED during this task's own live smoke test, introduced by this task's own first draft — not a pre-existing platform issue**: calling `platform_overview()` *before* `resolve_generation_provider_from_env()` meant the providers list's `active` flags were computed from `os.environ` *before* `.env` had ever been lazily loaded into the process (a side effect of `resolve_generation_provider_from_env()`'s own `load_dotenv=True` default) — so in a fresh process that had never yet handled a real build, every cloud provider showed "Needs key" even when a real key was configured, while `activeNow` (computed second, after the dotenv load already happened as a side effect) correctly named the real provider. An honest, internally self-contradictory response, not a crash — caught only because the live smoke test used a freshly started agent-engine process, exactly the scenario a real first-time user would hit. Fixed by resolving `activeNow` first, then `platform_overview()` second, so both parts of one response see the same, fully-loaded environment — verified with `env -i` (a completely clean environment, guaranteeing no accidental prior dotenv load) both before (reproducing the bug: `groq`/`google-gemini` showed `active: false` despite real configured keys) and after (confirming the fix: both correctly `active: true`) the change. Gates: agent-engine `task verify` — **3,629 OK** (4 new tests: get/404-disabled/502-on-error/available-without-preview-wiring); control-plane `go build`/`vet`/`test` all green (48 tests, 3 new: providers auth/proxy-verbatim-no-debit/unreachable); console `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (20 routes, 2 new: `/api/providers`, `/settings`); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass. Live (real Docker control-plane rebuilt via `control-plane.sh verify`, real agent-engine Studio server in preview mode, freshly restarted after the fix): real `GET /api/providers` (via the console proxy) returned `activeNow: {"providerId":"groq","modelId":"openai/gpt-oss-120b"}`, 11 providers listed, `groq`/`google-gemini`/`ollama-local` all correctly `active: true`; real `GET /settings` rendered the live "Ready" callout and the correct provider table. **Cross-verified `activeNow` against a real build, not just its own internal consistency**: `POST /api/jobs/build` with "A simple todo app" produced a real build whose own agent-engine log showed real Groq rate-limit retries (`groq is rate-limited (429); waiting...`) — confirming the real call genuinely went to Groq, exactly matching `activeNow`'s own report. Unauthenticated `GET /api/providers` → `401`; unauthenticated `GET /settings` → `307` redirect to `/login`, same as `/studio`'s own auth gate. NEXT (per the founder's "complete one by one all"): scope and fix the dynamic-route slug-collision codegen bug found live during R-481's own smoke test (a Next.js dynamic-route slug-name collision, "counterId" vs "counter_id", introduced by the edit-delta path). After that, R-483 (real-time build/edit streaming), per-user backend multi-tenancy, and Publish/deploy each involve a materially different architecture decision (transport protocol, tenancy model, deploy target) requiring explicit founder direction before implementation, per the standing "stop and ask for hard-gate architecture decisions" rule.

2026-09-19  R-481  Tabbed workspace — the SEVENTH AND FINAL task of the founder-approved 7-task Phase D roadmap (R-475–R-481). Restructured `/studio`'s main pane into four real tabs — Preview, Files, Code, Problems — with chat persisting alongside (not as a tab itself), assembling R-474 (files), R-477 (chat), R-479 (preview), and R-480 (problems) into one shell. New `app/studio/studio-tabs.tsx` owns `activeTab` and a lifted `selectedFile` shared by Files and Code, kept as two separate real tabs per the founder's explicit decision: **Files** is a lightweight clickable list — clicking a file sets `selectedFile` and switches to Code, so Files stays fast/simple for a non-technical user while still leading somewhere real; **Code** is the full syntax-highlighted reader with its own `<select>` file switcher too, for a technical user who wants to jump straight to reading code without detouring through Files first. New `app/studio/code-highlight.ts`: a hand-rolled, dependency-free tokenizer porting the *approach* (not the exact generated code) of `codegen/nextjs.py`'s own generated `tokenizeCodeLine`/`normalizeLang` — the same regex-driven, single-pass-per-line algorithm the platform already ships (tested) inside every generated app's own `CodeBlock` component, scoped to what a generated app actually contains: TypeScript/TSX/JavaScript (full keyword/type highlighting), JSON/CSS (generic string/number/punctuation splitting), Markdown and unknown extensions (plain text). Matches the R-475 icon-decision precedent — a small, fixed highlighting surface doesn't clear the bar for a new dependency, confirmed live that the documented `prismjs` fallback was not needed. **Problems tab** is an explicit on-demand "Check for problems" button (per R-480's own design, never automatic), hydrates the last stored report on mount via a `GET` so re-opening the tab doesn't lose prior results, shows real toolchain/no-web-target errors verbatim in the error banner rather than a generic failure. `studio-workspace.tsx` lost its inline `FileBrowser` (moved into the Files/Code tabs) — keeps just the name/description/stats/entities/usage header, now sitting above the tab bar. New `lib/control-plane.ts` additions (`ProblemsReport` type, `checkBuildProblems()`, `getBuildProblems()`) and a new proxy route `app/api/jobs/build/[id]/problems/route.ts` (GET+POST in one file). Wired into `studio-chat.tsx`: `<StudioTabs>` replaces the previous direct `<StudioPreview>` placement inside the same workspace column. Gates: console `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (19 routes, 1 new); `task verify` — **3,625 OK** (unchanged, no backend files touched by this task); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass. Not fixed here (out of scope for tab assembly); worth its own future Tracker ID once scoped. A repeated `GET` returned the byte-identical cached problems report in 17ms. `GET /studio?build=1` rendered the real `studio-tabs`/`tab-bar`/`tab-button` markup with all four tab labels present and no server errors. **THIS COMMIT COMPLETES THE APPROVED 7-TASK PHASE D ROADMAP (R-475 THROUGH R-481).** The Studio is now a real chat-driven, multi-pane workspace — persistent chat plus a tabbed Preview/Files/Code/Problems main pane — genuinely closer to Lovable/Dyad/Emergent parity than at the start of this roadmap. 2026-09-19  R-480  Backend — Problems/compile-report support. Sixth of the founder-approved 7-task plan (R-475–R-481). Real compile-error reporting for the first time in this codebase — the founder's explicit choice over a placeholder, even though it made the roadmap longer. Confirmed by exhaustive grep during the original planning research: `verify/compile.py` has a real `CompileError`/`CompileReport`/`compile_web_project()`, but its only caller anywhere in the codebase was a standalone CLI script — never the Studio's own `_build()`/`_edit()` path. Deliberately **on-demand, not automatic**: `node_modules`/`tsc` only exist in a generated repo after a live-preview install (confirmed by this session's own R-478/R-479 smoke tests, which needed a real `pnpm install` before any Node toolchain existed) — automating a compile check on every build/edit would make the fast, network-light core loop slower and more toolchain-dependent, a real regression this task avoids. New `services/agent-engine/.../studio/problems.py` mirrors `files.py`'s shape exactly: `check_build_problems(root_dir, runner=None)` resolves `root_dir/apps/web` (the same convention `localrun/plan.py`'s `build_run_plan` already uses to detect a web target), raises `NoWebTargetError` if there's no `package.json` there, calls the real `compile_web_project()`, and remaps its `VerifyError` (raised when `node_modules/.bin/tsc` doesn't exist) into a clear, actionable `ToolchainNotInstalledError` — never silently installs dependencies as a side effect of a "check problems" click. `runner` passes straight through to `compile_web_project`, mirroring `verify/compile.py`'s own test style, so this module's own tests need no real toolchain. `StudioProblemsStore` is a bounded, thread-safe, in-memory per-build-id LRU cache mirroring `StudioSessionStore`'s own idiom, so a repeated `GET` doesn't have to recompile. New agent-engine routes `POST`/`GET /api/build/{id}/problems` (mirroring the existing `_build_id_for_suffix` + handler pattern used for `/files`/`/file`/`/turns`/`/edit`), wired into `live_serve.py`'s always-on `control_kwargs` (build-only mode included) — checking/reading problems needs the build's own already-installed `node_modules`/`tsc` from a *prior* live-preview install, but not a *currently running* preview, the same reasoning `edit_fn`/`turns_fn` already established. Error mapping: `BuildNotFoundError`→404, `NoWebTargetError`→400 (parallel to `EditNotSupportedError`'s exact semantics), `ToolchainNotInstalledError`→409 (a real resource-state conflict, a status code not otherwise used by this API surface so a client can distinguish "install dependencies first" from every other failure), `ProblemsNotCheckedError`→404 (`GET` only). New control-plane routes `POST`/`GET /jobs/build/{id}/problems`, same generic-proxy shape as every other Job API route, **no credit debit** (a local `tsc` run is not a model call), a new `defaultProblemsTimeout` constant (90s, above ordinary observed single-app compile times, well below `verify/compile.py`'s own much larger internal default of 600s meant for pathological cases this route doesn't need to wait out) on the write route. Gates: agent-engine `task verify` — **Ran 3,625 tests, OK** (21 new: 5 `CheckBuildProblemsTests`, 5 `StudioProblemsStoreTests`, 4 `LiveServeProblemsWiringTests`, 8 new HTTP-level route tests — all against an injected fake `tsc` runner or a fresh tempdir, no real toolchain/subprocess needed in the automated suite); control-plane `go build`/`vet`/`test` all green (45 tests, 7 new); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass. Live (real Docker control-plane rebuilt via `control-plane.sh verify`, real agent-engine Studio server in both build-only and preview mode): build-only mode with no toolchain installed → real `409 {"error":"tsc is not installed for this app; run \`pnpm install --ignore-scripts\`... first"}` and a real `404 {"error":"...has not been checked for problems yet"}` before any check — the clear, actionable error this task's design specifically avoids turning into a crash or a silent multi-minute install. Along the way, hit two real, pre-existing environment issues honestly worked through rather than hidden, neither a bug in this task's own code: one preview build hit a real generated-migration collision (`ERROR: relation "bookmark_tag" already exists`, a codegen/migration bug in a many-to-many-heavy generated schema), another's preview page 500'd at runtime — neither blocked the actual test, since a problems check only needs the build's already-installed `node_modules`, not a *working* running preview. Preview mode with a real installed toolchain (confirmed `node_modules/apps/web/node_modules/.bin/tsc` genuinely present on disk) → a real `tsc --noEmit` run surfaced **10 genuine TypeScript errors** in an LLM-synthesized `app/page.tsx` (a missing `@/components/button` module, several not-callable-expression errors on a toast helper, a type mismatch on a `Sidebar` button prop, a missing `Avatar` name, an invalid `AlertVariant` value) — real, substantial, actionable output from the real compiler, not a synthetic fixture, and the same real bug explains why that build's own preview page was 500ing at runtime. Caching confirmed live: a repeated `GET` returned in 12ms, byte-identical to the original `POST` response — the stored report, not a recompile. Unauthenticated calls → `401`; `POST` against an unknown build id → real `404`. NEXT: R-481 (tabbed workspace) — the final task of the approved 7-task roadmap.

2026-09-19  R-479  Console — live preview UI. Fifth of the founder-approved 7-task plan (R-475–R-481). An iframe rendering the real running generated app, wired to R-478's four routes. New `app/studio/studio-preview.tsx`: one effect triggers `POST /jobs/build/{id}/preview` whenever `buildId` becomes real or a `previewVersion` counter (bumped by `studio-chat.tsx` after every successful edit — `_edit()` never restarts the preview on its own, unlike `_build()`) changes; a second effect runs the 5s crash-detection poll; a third ticks elapsed time while starting so the spinner shows real progress (cold starts confirmed live up to ~45s). A 404 from either preview route sets a `disabled` flag and renders an honest static message instead of a broken iframe. Manual Restart/Stop controls wired to the real R-478 routes, reusing the `RefreshIcon`/`StopIcon` that existed unused since R-475, purpose-built for exactly this. New `lib/control-plane.ts` additions (`PreviewStatus` type — every status shape verified by reading `preview.py` directly before implementation, not assumed — plus `getPreviewStatus`/`stopPreview`/`restartPreview`/`previewBuild` clients) and four new proxy routes (`app/api/preview`, `.../stop`, `.../restart`, `app/api/jobs/build/[id]/preview`). Wired into `studio-chat.tsx` via a new `previewVersion` state, rendered inside a new `.studio-workspace-column` alongside the existing `StudioWorkspace` panel inside `.studio-grid`. A real lint finding surfaced during implementation, not a design change: `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`'s `set-state-in-effect` rule flagged a synchronous `setState` call at the top of the buildId-change effect (the classic React-docs "set loading, then fetch" pattern) — fixed by moving it to be the first statement inside the effect's own async IIFE instead of before it, and consolidating `starting`/`elapsedMs`/`fetchError` into one combined state object so there is exactly one `setState` call there, not several cascading ones. Then: a real build immediately triggered the exact `POST /jobs/build/{id}/preview` call `<StudioPreview>`'s effect makes, returning a real `200 {"status":"ready","web_url":...}` whose `web_url`, fetched directly, returned genuine Next.js-rendered HTML — proof the iframe would show a real working app, not a blank frame; a real edit produced a genuine second commit, immediately followed by the exact re-preview call the `previewVersion` bump triggers, landing on a **new** port — confirming fresh, edited code was served, not stale content. Manual Restart recovered cleanly on a new port; manual Stop worked. Switching the agent-engine to build-only mode made both preview routes return the real, uniform `404 {"error":"preview controls are not enabled"}` through the console proxy — exactly the status `<StudioPreview>` checks for to render the honest disabled message. 2026-09-19  R-478  Backend — live preview proxy (local-only). Fourth of the founder-approved 7-task plan (R-475–R-481). Python side (`studio/live_serve.py`): `_edit()` now threads a real `UsageLedger` through `resolve_generation_provider_from_env`, adding a real `"usage"` key to both its response branches; `_build()` now calls `session_store.record_turn` for its own user/assistant turn. Turning the sketch into an executable plan went through full plan-mode discipline: two parallel Explore agents researched the real console-web frontend and the real agent-engine/control-plane backend (not the doc's assumptions), a Plan agent synthesized a task-by-task design, and the most consequential claims were independently verified by directly reading the source rather than trusted at face value — confirmed: `_build()` never records a chat turn though `_edit()` does (so a future chat hydrating history from `/turns` after a refresh would silently lose the first message); `_edit()` has no `usage_ledger` at all today, so every edit currently debits 0 credits regardless of real cost; the agent-engine's live-preview API is two different surfaces (a singleton and a build-scoped one), with the build-scoped route returning an unusual `200 {"status":"error"}` for an unknown build rather than a 404; the control-plane's `WriteTimeout` defaults to 15s against a real 45s preview-readiness wait; no compile/verify-error endpoint exists anywhere in the Studio path today (the only caller of the existing `compile_web_project()`/`compile_and_repair()` machinery is a standalone CLI script, never the Studio's own build/edit path). Two decisions were asked of the founder directly and both honored: Problems (compile-error reporting) gets built for real rather than deferred as a placeholder — given its own task, R-480, rather than bloating the tab-assembly task, which is what took the roadmap from six tasks to seven; Files and Code stay as two separate, real tabs (a lightweight browse/tree view and a full syntax-highlighted reader) rather than being collapsed into one. The plan was approved via `ExitPlanMode` twice this session — the session briefly re-entered plan mode mid-execution, after R-475's own contract and doc files were already written on disk; rather than re-litigating a plan that hadn't changed, a status note recording the real on-disk progress was added to the plan file and it was re-approved. R-475 itself: new `app/studio/layout.tsx` (a Server Component) takes over `/studio`'s auth gate (moved out of `page.tsx` so every future Studio sub-view inherits it for free) and renders persistent chrome — a slim top bar with the product brand, a back-to-home link, and the existing sign-out button — plus a `{children}` slot; no left sidebar, since there is nothing else inside Studio to navigate to yet. `app/globals.css` gained additive design tokens, mirrored into the existing `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` block exactly like every token before it: `--radius-sm/md/lg` (replacing today's inconsistent inline 8px/12px/14px radius values across `.field input`/`.field textarea`, `.button`, `.error-banner`, `.stat`, and `.panel`), `--surface-2` (an elevated tone distinct from `--panel`, for future nested cards), an accent-tinted chip background/foreground pair, a CSS-only `.spinner` (`@keyframes spin`, zero dependency, reusable by every later task's pending/loading states), and a `.pill--accent` modifier on the existing `.pill` for a persistent credit-balance chip. New `app/studio/studio-icons.tsx` ships roughly ten small hand-rolled inline-SVG stroke icons rather than adding an icon library — a deliberate decision recorded in the task contract: the fixed, small icon surface needed across the whole seven-task roadmap doesn't clear the bar for a new dependency when the in-house alternative is this cheap and everything else in this app is already hand-rolled CSS/SVG. `studio-form.tsx` was restyled only, with zero logic change — the credit balance moved from its own dedicated stat panel into a compact `.pill--accent` chip near the top of the page. No control-plane or agent-engine changes; no new chat/tab/preview functionality — this task is purely the shell and design tokens later tasks build on. Gates: `pnpm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (13 routes, unchanged from R-474 — this task adds no new route); `task verify` — **Ran 3,603 tests, OK, Stage 0 verification passed**; `task lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass; a new `scripts/test.sh` R-475 contract block confirms the new files exist, the auth gate moved into `layout.tsx`, and the new design tokens/classes are present in `globals.css`. **Live verification**: Colima/Docker had stopped since the previous session and was restarted (`colima start`); a real Docker Compose Postgres+control-plane (`bash scripts/control-plane.sh verify`), a real agent-engine Studio server, and a fresh `next start` on the new build proved `GET /studio` with no session now redirects via `layout.tsx`'s own auth gate (`307` to `/login`, same as before this refactor); registering a fresh account and requesting `/studio` again returned `200` with the new shell markup present (`class="studio-shell"`, `class="studio-topbar"`, the "OmniStackAI Studio" brand text) and the credit pill correctly rendering the real balance (`100<!-- --> credits` — the HTML comment is React's own harmless hydration boundary between two adjacent JSX text nodes, the identical pattern already diagnosed as a false alarm back in R-471, not a new bug); `/`, `/fabric`, `/login`, and `/register` all still returned `200` with their existing structure completely intact (the home page's real `<h1>Welcome back, R475 Smoke</h1>` heading, one `.panel`, `.wrap`/`.masthead` both present) — confirming the shared `.panel` radius-token change is the only visible effect on those pages, exactly as designed. `package.json`'s `dependencies` were confirmed still exactly `next`/`react`/`react-dom`, verifying the no-new-dependency decision held. This ships the smallest real, end-to-end slice — prompt in, real build out, real credit debit reflected in the UI — with the file browser, live preview, and chat/multi-turn edit named as follow-ups, not silently dropped. New `app/studio/page.tsx` (Server Component, mirrors `app/page.tsx`'s auth gate exactly — `getCurrentUser()` redirects to `/login` when signed out); new `app/studio/studio-form.tsx` (Client Component: a prompt textarea, a Build button, a real pending state since builds take real time — 3 seconds to 3-plus minutes observed live, both in this task and R-472's — a result panel rendering name/description/entities/file_count/commit_sha/files plus the `usage` block and `credits_spent` when the agent-engine reported them, and an error banner on failure); new `app/api/jobs/build/route.ts` (server-side proxy: reads the session cookie via `getSessionToken()`, 401s immediately with **no** upstream call when it is absent, then forwards the request body to the control-plane's `POST /jobs/build` with the bearer token attached — never exposed to client-side JavaScript, the exact boundary every existing `app/api/auth/*` route already draws; no artificial timeout, since R-472's own `http.ResponseController.SetWriteDeadline` fix is what makes waiting out a real multi-minute build possible in the first place). `lib/control-plane.ts` gained `BuildJobResponse`/`BuildJobUsage`/`buildApp()`, following the exact `callControlPlane` pattern `login`/`registerAccount` already use — only the fields this UI actually renders are typed, with an index signature so other build-kind-specific fields (e.g. `pack_id`) still round-trip without the UI needing to know about them. `app/page.tsx` gained a link to `/studio`; `app/globals.css` gained a `.field textarea` rule (only `input` was styled before) and small `.pill-row`/`.pill`/`.file-list` treatments for the result panel, reusing every existing design token — no new UI dependency. No changes to `services/control-plane` or `services/agent-engine`: R-472 already built the real backend this task's UI calls. Gates: `pnpm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (11 routes, with `/studio` and `/api/jobs/build` both correctly dynamic since they read the session cookie per request); `task verify` — **Ran 3,603 tests, OK, Stage 0 verification passed**; `task lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass; a new `scripts/test.sh` R-473 contract block confirms the new files exist, `buildApp()` is defined, and the build route checks `getSessionToken()` before proxying. **Live verification**: real Docker Compose Postgres+control-plane (`bash scripts/control-plane.sh verify`), a real agent-engine Studio server on local Ollama (`OMNISTACKAI_PREFER_LOCAL=1`, kept free and reproducible matching R-472's precedent), and a real `next start` console proved: `GET /studio` with no session cookie → `307` to `/login`; with a real session cookie → `200`, the server-rendered page showing the correct credit balance; `POST /api/jobs/build` with no cookie → `401 {"error":"not signed in"}` instantly, with no upstream call attempted. The second attempt (a different prompt) succeeded for real: a genuine 157-file "Recipe Box" repository (`entities: ["Recipe", "User"]`, a real `commit_sha`, a real `files` list, `usage: {cost_micros_usd: 0, total_calls: 2, successful_calls: 1, failed_calls: 1, ...}`, `credits_spent: 0`, `credit_balance: 100`) — correct (local Ollama stays credit-exempt) and an exact field-for-field match for everything `BuildResult` in `studio-form.tsx` renders.
